Wanda’s mouth opened and closed like a nutcracker. How was she even supposed to respond? “This is an insane theory,” she landed on.

“And yet I have a gut feeling about it,” Bella said, then smirked wildly. “Get it?”

“So this is why you want access to my lab,” Wanda scoffed. “So you can test your magic fungi theory?”

Bella completely ignored her condescension, smirk still firmly on her face. “You throw around the word magic like it’s something you don’t believe exists.”

Wanda paused, gobsmacked, watching as Bella finished with her foundation, then started filling in her lips, so they were that vibrant, terrifying crimson.

“I’m a scientist,” Wanda said. “Of course I don’t believe in magic. I wouldn’t be studying immortality if I thought it was something that could only be given to you by some magic force.”

Bella rolled her eyes, shooting her a knowing smile. What she thought she knew, Wanda couldn’t say. Bella capped her lipstick, tucking it away.

“Sure, of course,” Bella said. “Yes. You’re correct. This is why I wanted access to your lab. You have DNA sequencing machines, anaerobic chambers, oh, and a shit ton of money for anything else I’d need. It would help my research considerably.”

“But that’s not what we agreed to. I hired you to work on the subject of immortality. Not to mention, you promised we’d complete that research in a month.”

Bella’s eyes drifted toward her in the reflection of the small makeup mirror. Wanda gripped the edge of the desk harder as she forced herself to stand her ground.

“I sympathize with what you’re going through, and under other circumstances, I’d probably love to study your condition myself,” Wanda said quietly. “But if you want to work with me and use my equipment, we’ll be working on my thesis, not yours.”

Bella snapped the mirror closed.

“Sorry, I think I’ve confused you,” she said, sighing. She looked at her nails, studying the red sheen for several long seconds, as if she was thinking about her next words, before she fixed Wanda with a look. “There’s one other symptom about my condition that I didn’t mention that makes it very relevant to your research.”

Taking a step forward, she pressed her hands to Wanda’s shirt, ironing out the wrinkles. Wanda swallowed, her thoughts reeling.

Bella bit her lip, looking down at Wanda with nervous eyes.

“You have to make me a few promises before I tell you,” she said, a genuine vulnerability in her voice.

Wanda hated how she instantly felt like she wanted to protect Bella from whatever it was she was afraid of.

“Okay,” Wanda said, taking a breath in. “I can try.”

“Well first, you can’t tell anyone about this… symptom. Even your friends.”

Wanda blinked, surprised. That wouldn’t be hard. All she cared about was the end result, sharing the details of how they got there wouldn’t be necessary.

“Easy. What else?”

Bella’s fingers clenched around the fabric of Wanda’s shirt. She couldn’t meet her eyes. It was incredibly off-character.

“You need to keep up your end of the deal. If we finish the research within a month, you still owe me the money.”

“Um, duh?”

Wanda tapped her foot, growing impatient. If Bella was just wasting her time again…

“And lastly, you can’t ask me any questions about my condition outside the scope of our research,” she said, letting out a shaky breath as she finally released Wanda’s shirt. “Deal?”

These details seemed completely insignificant to Wanda, who just shrugged and said, “Sure, what the hell. It’s not like you ever answer any of my questions anyways. Deal.”

Bella swallowed. “Thank you,” she said, voice strained.

“So?” Wanda said, pushing Bella lightly backwards at her hips, so there was some distance between them. “What’s next, do you need me to sign an NDA?”

Bella laughed, finally regaining some of that lightness back to her.

“No. With the kind of legal team I’m sure you have, I’m pretty sure that would just backfire on me. Guilt tripping you for breaking a promise is way more effective.”

Wanda rolled her eyes. “Probably. Now out with it. What’s your secret?”

Bella nodded. She took a step back, sat back down on the gurney, and looked at Wanda through strands of disorderly blonde.

“Well, it’s no big deal, really. I wouldn’t say it’s a problematic age gap. It’s just that…” Bella trailed off, then bit her lip. “I’m older than you.”

Chapter 17

Chapter Summary

The one where the truth comes out

Wanda blinked. “Older than me?”

Publicly, Wanda was thirty-six. May 5th, 1989 was the date of birth on her latest forged passport. Physically and cellularly, it was a pretty decent approximation. Bella looked, well… Her eyes scanned over Bella’s poreless, wrinkle-free face. Even with the bruises, she looked like a fountain of youth. Early thirties at the very most.

Also, Bella’s high school diploma stated that she was the class of 2014. The great majority of people—even eccentric prodigies—graduated high school right around the time they turned eighteen. Which would make Bella exactly thirty years old.

“First of all,” Wanda said, crossing her arms. “You’re absolutely not older than me. Second, what the hell does your age have to do with your … fungal symptoms?”

“No, I’m really serious,” Bella said, pouting. “I am older than you, Wanda.”

“Like hell you are. I’m thirty-six.”

Bella sneered, covering her face to conceal her laughter.

“And I thought I was bad at math,” she said. “You were born in 829 AD. That would make you…” Bella began to count on her fingers. “1,197 years old.”

Wanda blinked, her next words dying in her throat as the color drained from her face. The car jerked right, and she had to fight to stay upright, her hands clawing around the table at her back. Bella, meanwhile, maintained her balance just fine.

829 AD. That wasn’t some kind of loose guess one could excuse as a joke. That was the exact year Wanda was born.

An extreme sense of unease settled in her stomach as she tried to make sense of what Bella just said to her. Don’t jump to conclusions, she reminded herself. This girl was smart, resourceful, and very good at making Wanda feel like she was going insane.

But figuring out that Wanda was a billionaire through some careful internet sleuthing was one thing, tracking her back that far through time, discerning her actual age, that was impossible.

There was no paper trail that went back that far.

No. What? No. There’s no fucking way.

But what other option was there?

To know her real birthday, you’d have to have been alive back then.

You’d have to be a vampire. Or a vampire’s thrall.

“We’re approaching your residence, Ms. Maximoff,” Ashley shouted from the front, but her voice registered in Wanda’s ears as a dull ringing.

The windowless van felt suddenly like a coffin. The air felt humid, chalky. Her hands became so clammy that they could barely hold on to the table.

But crawling out from beneath that fear, that shock and confusion, was anger. Before she knew what she was doing, she took Bella by the front of her turtleneck and jerked her head forward. She was seething, her fingertips trembling.

“Who do you belong to?” Wanda spat, her voice cracking.

Wanda had many enemies. Much fewer after Agatha and Rio’s spontaneous slaughter of the council, but they still existed—nightwalkers who coveted her immense wealth, or, far more annoyingly, the ones who coveted the artifacts in her collection. She had never considered a vampire who would be smart enough to covet her research.

“Belong to?” Bella said with a light laugh, raising her hand to hold Wanda’s curled, reddening fingers. “Do you take me for a thrall?”

A thrall. Hearing the words fall so easily out of Bella’s mouth solidified Wanda’s horrible paranoia. She could hardly breathe. Couldn’t think. She felt nauseous. Wanda felt like a pirate who’d walked himself off the gangplank at the mere sight of a siren. She’d been tricked. She’d seen it coming from a hundred miles out, and yet.

“If you’re not a thrall…” Wanda said, jerking Bella closer despite the tidal wave of bile in her throat. The other woman let out a choked breath. Wanda tried not to care if she was hurting her. “Then why are you telling me this now? You could have fooled me for another few months. You could have stolen my research and left.”

“Oh, honey,” Bella laughed, even as she began to slowly asphyxiate under her turtleneck. “There was really nothing to steal.”

That honey stung. To think that Wanda had really believed Bella wanted them to be friends. How foolish and stupid and childish it felt now, that she’d wanted it, too.

A mirthless smile spread across Wanda’s face.

“Funny.”

She shoved Bella backwards with enough force to shatter a bone, surging the gurney straight into the opposite wall. Then Wanda turned back towards the medical cabinets, taking a deep breath in to center herself as the gurney slammed into the side of the armored truck, causing the vehicle to veer.

“Christ! Watch it!” Rebecca shouted, rapidly spinning the wheel to compensate.

Dr. Larson’s face turned toward the commotion, the shadow of his head visible through the small window partition separating the two halves of the van.

“Ms. Maximoff?” he called out. “Do you need my assistance?”

“No, no, stay put,” Wanda muttered. “Everything’s perfect back here.”

She turned back towards Bella, who, as she expected, hadn’t been thrown off by even an inch. There were no new bruises on her collarbones where Wanda had pushed her. No, she was completely fine; in fact, the blonde hopped off the now-misshapen gurney with all the casualness of someone rising out of a bus seat.

That push should have done something to her, Wanda thought, with a small pulse of fear; and what was funny was that fear wasn’t new. She’d been irrationally afraid of Bella ever since she met her, only now she had a reason to be.

How quickly she’d gone from wanting to protect this woman to wanting to protect herself from her.

“I’m sorry, Wanda. I really didn’t mean to upset you,” Bella laughed, and the tinkling, still- charming sound of it made Wanda want to fling her into the wall again. “I’m just nervous. I wasn’t sure you’d take the news well.”

Take the news well. As if this was Bella announcing that she was opening a fucking restaurant.

They met again in the middle of the room, this time without a large metal obstruction between them. Wanda tried to look her straight in the eyes unflinchingly when she asked, “I don’t care what the hell you’re feeling. What’s your family name?”

Bella’s face fell, and she threw up a hand. “I plead the third.”

“It’s the fifth,” Wanda sputtered.

Bella rolled her eyes. “I’m not talking about this silly country’s constitutional amendments. I’m talking about our vastly more important agreement. Stipulation three. You can’t ask me anything about my condition outside the scope of the research.”

At that, Wanda’s rage boiled down into pure astonishment.

“When I agreed to that, I had no idea your condition would be vampirism.”

“Well, then it’s your fault you made an agreement before you had the full picture. You can’t blame me for that.”

Wanda pushed against Bella’s folded arms, this time without nearly as much force. “I can quite explicitly blame you for that!”

Bella giggled, and it sounded to Wanda like someone was stabbing her with pretty little knives, like she was Caesar on the Ides of March.

Because this human woman, this brilliant scientist who’d been tormenting her for months, was a vampire. A vampire. The revelation simply wouldn’t digest. She felt it stuck in her throat; she was choking on it.

“I still don’t believe you,” Wanda said. “I’ve never seen your eyes go red.”

“Of course you haven’t. I’ve never used my powers on you.”

“And those are?”

Bella poked her tongue into her cheek. “Not relevant.”

Wanda’s mouth opened and closed, completely aghast. Angry. Pissed off. Furious. This woman gave her an inch, then made her work for a mile. It shouldn’t have been like that. Wanda was the one in control. Wanda could have her killed on a dime.

No, she could do worse than that. Wanda could make Bella relive the worst moments of her life over and over, for the rest of her questionably immortal existence. All it would take is a blink of her eyelashes, and Bella would be reduced to the little girl trapped in the closet.

And yet, God—God—Wanda knew she couldn’t do that.

Bella had buried under her skin. Had that been her plan all along? To make Wanda like her so much as a human, as some innocent non-offensive little mortal creature, that she’d be too enamored to kill her once she learned the truth?

That was scary. Because if that was Bella’s plan, that meant Wanda would have a reason to want her dead. A reason that went beyond the relationship they had now.

“Bella,” Wanda said quietly. “You could have told me you were a vampire the day we met. You obviously knew back then. You could have told me any day since. You haven’t. You kissed me, fucked me—” Wanda’s voice faltered. That didn’t matter. Didn’t matter at all. Why was she bringing it up? “You lied to my face. Lying vampires are a dangerous breed. I don’t keep them in my circle. I hardly keep them alive.”

Sensing that she meant what she said, Bella’s face finally fell. She swallowed, and for the first time, Wanda saw a hint of real nervousness under the facade.

“Nothing has changed,” she said quietly, her eyes flitting up to hold Wanda’s gaze. “All I want to do is help you out with your research. Something is different in my biology, different from yours, and most other vampires. It’s like a… defect. It’s what causes these.” Bella held up her arm. “And it is a fungal problem. That’s all true.”

Slowly, Bella put her arm back down, rolling her sleeve over the bruise.

“I wasn’t trying to play games with you,” she said softly. “I really thought the only way I’d convince you to work with me was if you didn’t know. Which—well—that was probably the correct instinct, seeing how you’re responding right now. And I didn’t have sex with you as some sort of ploy. I did it because I find you….”

Wanda’s mouth went dry, feeling speechless as Bella’s eyes lingered too long on her face. The blonde cleared her throat, not finishing her sentence.

“The only reason I’m telling you now is because I think, seeing what you just did for me…” Bella bit her lip. “I have this foolish instinct that I can trust you.”

Wanda’s chest fluttered, but her disbelief overrode the feeling.

“I’m sorry—you must be kidding—that you can trust me?”

“Well, yes? What makes you more trustworthy than me?” Bella asked flatly, raising an eyebrow. “What makes you less likely to betray me than I am to betray you?”

Wanda scoffed. “Well, I’ve been upfront about everything, for one.”

“Sure you have. And I’m sure you’ve been upfront with all the people you’ve eventually had maimed and killed. That’s just how you are, Wanda. It doesn’t make you inherently less dangerous.”

That drew a rash laugh of disbelief out of Wanda. She couldn’t necessarily dispute it.

“You won’t even tell me your full name,” she shot back.

Bella shrugged. “You wouldn’t recognize it even if I did.”

“Impossible. There’s not a vampire clan on Earth I don’t know about.”

“Yes, well,” Bella swallowed again, looking like she was on the verge of something. “We were a bit before your time. About a thousand years before your time.”

Wanda’s mouth fell agape, stunned.

After a beat, Wanda finally found the words, but as she was about to speak, the siren of a fire truck whizzed by them, blaring like hell. It was immediately followed by another, then another. A cacophony of screaming whistles drowning out every other sound. Bella’s expression turned from thoughtful to scared.

Her eardrums splintering, Wanda snapped her head toward the driver’s seat. “What the hell is going on out there?”

“Fire,” Ashley said, but it didn’t contain her usual sassy apathy. “Ms. Maximoff, the entire block is burning.” She turned toward Wanda. “Including your house.”

Chapter 18

Chapter Summary

The one where Bella walks into a house fire. Also the one where Bella meets Rio Vidal.

Smoke was wafting up from Central Park West like steam from a boiling pot. Jumping down from the van, Wanda brought her sleeve up to cover her mouth, but she still found it hard to breathe. The sweet plastic smell was burning up her nostrils.

She squinted through the smog. It was almost impossible to make out her house. All that was visible were charred patches of wood falling off the sides of it like shredded kebab meat. A huddle of firemen pushed past her, sprinting toward it.

If she were a human being, she probably would have felt sad. But Wanda had learned better than to become attached to sentimental objects like houses. All her items and artifacts with actual value were distributed across the globe in various vaults, better protected than any president. All this house had was some pictures of her and Billy, eclectic eighteenth century art, and some beautiful chandeliers.

She scowled when she felt a tear streak down her face nonetheless.

Get a hold of yourself.

She swiped it away, then turned to Rebecca. “What am I looking at? Is it arson?”

Rebecca shot her a half-pitying frown, and Wanda considered firing her for it, but her assistant knew better than to keep that kind of expression on her face for long. She quickly schooled it into something more sensible.

“Most certainly,” Rebecca said. “There’s been a string of identical house fires in the past month. This would make the third.”

“Several?” Wanda’s obsession with Bella really had kept her out of the loop. “And they haven’t found the perpetrators yet? There’s no video footage?”

New York City was full of street cameras. Committing a crime on a public street, especially a crime as vibrant as arson, seemed like it’d be difficult to do completely out of view of the surveillance state.

That’s why she always advised Agatha to try and restrict her murderous impulses to private, unsupervised places. Not that she ever listened.

“No. There’s plenty of footage. All the tapes feature these two young women, but they’re always wearing masks.”

“Masks? Like ski masks?”

“No, no. Think masquerade ball.”

“Just kill me,” Bella groaned as she hopped out of the van, pushed past Wanda, and headed towards the fire. The delayed part of Wanda’s mind immediately worried about her feeble little human lungs, but then she remembered.

“Where do you think you’re going?” Wanda called out, jogging after her.

They both disappeared into the smog, and even though Wanda’s lungs could filter air better than her mortal counterparts, they still weren't miracle workers. She covered her mouth and blinked through the burning air as she tried to keep the shape of Bella in her sights.

“Don’t follow me!” Bella shouted. “Just wait outside until the smoke clears!”

“As if!” Wanda shouted back. “Whatever lipstick you left in there is not worth cooking yourself alive for!”

The heat grew more oppressive as she neared the house. Wanda had no idea how Bella was walking so brazenly through it, but she was; she stepped through the ashes of the front door, shouldering past a fireman who stared after her in complete horror.

Wanda scowled. What the hell does she think she’s doing, exposing herself like this? It wasn’t easy to explain away a woman strutting through a burning building in blue jeans. God, did she have another Agatha on her hands? She’d assumed Bella would be less reckless, with all the effort she’d put in keeping her identity a secret so far…

“Sorry about this. You really don’t deserve it,” Wanda said, facing the fireman. She looked him right in his baby blue eyes, holding his gaze until it darkened. Every person reacted differently to their nightmare, but most screamed or ran or both. It was a testament to this man’s character that only his eye twitched.

Wanda waved over the other firemen in her smoldering foyer. “I think your friend is having a seizure. Best to get him out of here,” she shouted. They quickly came to help, one or two insisting that she get back outside, that she’d die if she inhaled any more of the air, and she agreed. She made a show of leaving, but once she was out of their line of vision, she slunk back inside, using the smoke as cover.

“Bella!” Wanda hissed as she stepped over cracked glass. She dared not look down, should she find the remains of her favorite Rembrandt. “Where are you?”

Silence. Even though she was growing dizzier by the second, she pushed into the living room. It was wrecked; the ceiling sagged so far downward that the chandelier dangled just above the floor. The couch was as black as an overcooked steak. There was still an active fire at the top of the stairs spewing heat.

Still, there was no sign of Bella on this floor. Had she actually gone upstairs? Was the girl suicidal? Vampires could withstand high temperatures much better than mortals, but walking through an active fire was a touch too far.

Wanda had experienced it herself. She’d lived in Massachusetts for a stint during the witch trials era—she enjoyed New England’s geography, less so its penchant for hanging women— and some dimwit teenager had mistaken her house for someone else’s, and tried to torch it. She’d survived, obviously, but the burn marks took weeks to heal. If she hadn’t been near a window, she’d probably have scars for life.

“Bella!” she shouted again, rearing around the staircase and taking an apprehensive step upward. She could hear someone talking up there. It was muffled by the gusts of wind blowing through the blown-out windows, but it was distinctive.

“Where are you two? Where are you hiding?” This time it wasn’t Wanda asking the question. It was Bella. She was shouting it over and over as she stalked the second floor. Wanda could hear her footsteps hectically crunching overhead. How was she doing that, pacing through the fire like she was on a phone call? Did she not feel any pain?

Groaning at the extreme heat, Wanda forced herself up the stairs. Once she was at the precipice, staring out at the crumpled stacks of charred wood that used to be her display tables and drawers and painting frames, she finally caught sight of Bella. The woman had stopped her incessant walking and yelling, and was now sitting on the floor, hands in her hair, seemingly frustrated. Flames were raging on all sides of her.

Wanda’s eyes widened. What the hell? The flames were raging, but they didn’t swallow Bella; on the contrary, the fire halted a few inches from where she was sitting. It was like watching a pack of trapped wolves salivating at a piece of meat. Whenever the fire dared to encroach a centimeter further, it quickly guttered and died, turning to soot.

Wanda thought the heat might have caused her to imagine it, but then Bella reached out, pushing her hand straight into the fire, and it recoiled. Any flame that grazed her fingertips immediately smoked out. Bella frowned, as if annoyed by the reaction, and tucked her hand back in.

“Is this your power?” Wanda choked out. “You’re what… the vampire version of firefighter foam?”

Bella’s head whipped in her direction, eyebrows flung high.

“You shouldn’t be in here,” Bella said, clambering to her feet. “You’re so red. You’re going to have a heat stroke. Or a heart attack.”

Wanda pushed Bella’s hand off her forehead.

“What else can you do?” Wanda said, ignoring her. “And who were you looking for?”

“People who clearly aren’t here anymore,” Bella said with an annoyed slant to her voice. She latched on to Wanda’s right arm. “I thought I might be able to catch those arsonists that Rebecca was telling you about, but it seems they’re gone.”

Wanda snorted. “If they were still up here, they would be corpses.”

Bella licked across her lips. “If only. Come on, let’s get you out of here.”

Bella hooked her arm around Wanda’s midsection, holding her up as they began to walk back down the stairs. Wanda wanted to swat her away in protest, but when she tried to, she felt a wave of dizziness pour over her. Her heart started pounding madly in her chest.

“Ah, there it is,” Wanda murmured, feeling her vision darken. “Heat stroke.”

“I told you, idiot.”

“Don’t call me idiot,” Wanda slurred. Oh, there went the last of her vision. Complete darkness now. At least she could still hear. “You’re the one who ran into the burning house in complete view of the public. If you come back outside without a blemish on you, people are going to talk. There might even be video footage. You could be on television, you big dumb vampire.”

Bella’s hand tightened around her waist. They were nearly to the bottom of the stairwell now. Wanda could tell because the heat was a lot less oppressive, and she could hear people shouting again. The crowd outside had definitely gotten larger.

“You’ll deal with it,” Bella said, much too cocky for Wanda’s liking. “What’s your money even good for if you can’t bribe your local news anchors?”

“Idiot,” Wanda repeated, because she was unable to say much else at that point.

Bella pinched her side. Wanda didn’t have to see her to know she was smiling.

***

Of all things, a baby woke her up.

One of Nicky’s feet was dangling just above her head, kicking her softly and repeatedly in the cheek. And no, he wasn’t being hoisted there by some mischievous adult, he was just there, levitating, holding a juice pouch and a picture book.

And because babies can only hold onto things for approximately three seconds before losing interest, the picture book was shortly released onto Wanda’s forehead. She hissed, scrubbing the quickly forming welt. That book was needlessly heavy.

“Sorry, those things are like mega dumbbells,” Rio said as Nicky began to float upward. “We had all his toys enchanted to be thirty times heavier than their normal weight so he stops flying out of windows.”

“Enchanted?” Wanda mumbled, slowly rising from the couch she’d been laid out on. God, her head hurt. “Since when do you all know how to enchant things?”

Rio gently yanked Nicky down before he could make a full ascent towards the ceiling. “Agatha needed new hobbies to distract herself from having thoughts.”

“Ah, healthy,” Wanda muttered. She hadn’t talked to Agatha in a while, now that she thought about it. Not after their little fight about the credit cards. “And where is this thoughtless woman?”

Wanda heard the water run in the bathroom. “On a work trip,” Rio answered.

“A work trip?” Now Wanda thought she really might be in some kind of coma-induced dream. “Agatha doesn’t work.”

The door to the bathroom opened, and Rio hastily pinned Nicky to her chest, so he couldn’t fly off anywhere. Wanda was confused by her urgency until she realized who had just come out of the bathroom.

It was Bella.

And everything came rushing back to Wanda. The fire. Bella’s confession. The last thing Wanda remembered, she was walking down the stairs back to her burnt foyer. And now she was here. Did Bella take her here? Why did she know where Agatha lived? Then again, she’d known enough about Agatha to know she was abusing Wanda’s credit cards. Of course she knew her address.

“I like your soap,” Bella said to Rio, as if this was a hotel. “It smells like… I don’t even know what it smells like. But it’s nice.”

“Thanks,” Rio said. “Agatha shoplifted it. Apparently it’s shark secretions.”

Bella snorted. Wanda watched them interact with her jaw slightly dislodged.

“I see you two are acquainted,” Wanda said, wincing as she pried herself off of the couch. “Bella, how did you even get me here?”

Bella gave her a bewildered look, as if that was a stupid question. “You have a personal driver. I just asked her where you’d ideally like to wake up after your house just burnt down, and she drove us here. Plus, it’s not like I had a place to take you.”

The sentimentality in that question made Wanda frown. She could admit that it had been comforting, waking up in Agatha’s apartment. A false sort of comfort, though. She could only romanticize it because Agatha wasn’t in town to piss her off. She’d left behind her much less offensive equivalent—the literal serial killer.

“Were you in the house while it was burning? Because you look like shit,” Rio said, sizing her up. Wanda looked down at her arms, wincing when she saw the burn marks. “I can’t believe you were targeted. Agatha would be having a field day with this. She’s been obsessed with those girls all week.”

Bella clicked her tongue. Wanda noticed only because she’d done it enough to register as a nervous tick. Rio finally sat down across from Wanda, the bright light of the windows illuminating her so she looked like an angel. Bella joined her on the couch. Given the positioning, Wanda felt suddenly like they were at couples therapy.

“Sort of,” Wanda said, making sure to keep a wide margin between her and Bella. She didn’t want to give Rio the wrong idea about them. “And of course Agatha’s obsessed. What has the illustrious idiot detective made of the situation? Has she figured out who they are yet?”

“Well, she seems to think they’re…” Rio paused, then stared at Bella. She made that awkward face that she always pulled when she had to lie. “...Supernaturally good at what they do.”

Oh, for the love of fuck.

It had only been a few minutes, and Wanda had already had enough of this circus. Rio was holding her tongue because she thought Bella was a human. Bella was refusing to say otherwise because she was pointlessly secretive about her identity. And Wanda wasn’t speaking up because she’d promised Bella that she wouldn’t divulge her secret to anyone else, per their stupid agreement.

Well, Wanda hadn’t signed any dotted line.

“Rio,” Wanda said, wearing the world’s tightest smile. “Bella is a vampire.”

She didn't look at Bella when she said it, but she could hear her jaw click open. She could also very much feel the temperature in the room increase.

Bella tried to do her usual thing of laughing it off. “Wanda, don’t be silly—”

Wanda didn’t even let her try and start the bullshit machine. She just grabbed Bella’s hand, then grabbed Rio’s, yanking them both forward towards each-other.

“Rio, this is Bella. She’s two thousand years old, can put out fires with her hands, and she’s been fucking with my mind for the last month and a half. Bella, this is Rio. She’s essentially impossible to kill, and she can separate your head from your neck in two seconds flat. So keep that in mind. So happy you two can finally meet!”

Chapter 19

Chapter Summary

A date at Central Park, if you squint

It was extremely annoying just how quickly Bella got over it.

Wanda had been hoping to piss her off even a little, but no. Miss Vampire Cool Girl just stared into space for half a second, collected herself, then shook Rio’s hand.

“Splendid to meet you,” she said. “Although I must admit I already know who you are. I’m a big fan of your work.”

Rio’s face rarely ever revealed much. But Wanda could tell she was surprised from the way she loosened her grip on Nicky, letting him float out of her hands.

“Thank you,” Rio enunciated slowly. Her eyes slid over to Wanda. “I feel like I missed a few chapters. Isn’t this your barista girl?”

Wanda’s cheeks reddened at the highly unnecessary use of the word your. Bella, not missing a beat, flashed her a wild smirk. “Is that how you’ve pitched me to your friends?” she asked, pushing at Wanda’s waist in a way that had to be retribution.

“So you two are together,” Rio said.

“Absolutely,” Bella said.

“Not in any way, shape, or form,” Wanda said simultaneously.

Bella barked out a laugh; Wanda looked at her in astonishment.

“Not in any way, shape, or form? I mean, that’s just a lie,” Bella said, positively delighted. “Don’t lie to your best friend, Wanda.”

Wanda blinked at her, dumbfounded. What the hell was she doing? Was this her attempt at wrestling back control of the situation? It didn’t feel like she’d lost any ground to begin with; she was sitting there like she owned the place, last night's charred outfit substituted for a pair of Rio’s black jeans and a Linkin Park hoodie, blonde hair swept into a messy bun.

Wanda swallowed. God, was she really still attracted to this pathological liar?

Her being a vampire changed nothing. If anything, it made their relationship even more untenable. At least when she was a human, she was harmless. She could be Wanda’s coworker and her fling, and the worst fallout they could have would be a screaming match at Wanda’s apartment.

Vampire break ups were a lot more like eighteenth century duels. Two gentlemen enter the ring, one leaves.

Not that any of that really mattered. Bella was just toying with her again, and she didn’t like Bella romantically regardless. She was, first of all, insane, and despite her age, completely petulant; Wanda was stable, and organized, and a mother.

“I’m not lying,” Wanda shot back. Rio didn’t seem convinced. Even Nicky had stopped babbling to crook his head down and listen. “Look, we slept together twice. Before I knew she was immortal. That does not constitute a romance. If it did, half the men in this city would be in committed relationships with fifteen different women.”

Rio laughed, taking a swig from the wine glass next to her. “So you two slept together! God, Agatha will be so pissed she missed this.”

Wanda rolled her eyes. Why was she justifying herself?

“Where is Agatha, anyway? Because I know she’s not working,” she pivoted.

Rio’s smirk dissolved. She set down her glass, and rummaged for something in her pocket. She took out her phone.

“Take a look for yourself.”

Confused, Wanda took the phone. It was open to a gallery of images—all photos Agatha had sent Rio in the last week. In sequential order: Agatha on a plane, drinking wine; Agatha on a sailboat, drinking wine; Agatha posing with an older Italian woman, with a vineyard full of ripe grapes in the background, presumably to be harvested and then ingested in the form of wine.

“That’s Lillia,” Rio commented dryly, pointing at the older woman. “Her Enchanting instructor. She made us these rings.”

Rio flashed the serpent ring around her finger. It moved slightly in the light.

“Enchanting instructor?” Wanda laughed. “She stuck you with a newborn while she fucked off to Sicily to learn how to turn baby books into heavier baby books?”

“It’s certainly post-partum something,” Rio said, leaning back in her chair to stare at Nicky, who was twirling himself in a circle and giggling. “I’m just letting it run its course.”

Wanda sighed. “You don’t have to take her shit, you know.”

Rio titled her head back towards her, her slick black bangs falling over brown eyes. She smiled; it had a warmth there that Wanda couldn’t quite grasp.

“What’s the saying? The pot is calling the kettle black?” Rio said, and the clunkiness of it only drew further attention to the slight color of her accent that still hadn’t eroded over the years. “Anyway, we haven’t even gotten to the gossip yet.”

“Our budding relationship isn’t good gossip?” Bella said, then pushed Wanda’s arm. “Honey, say something.”

What the hell is her problem? “Please go have your maladaptive daydreams in a different corner of the house,” Wanda muttered, blushing even harder as she turned her full attention to Rio. She put a hand up to keep Bella out of her peripheral vision. “So what, Agatha thinks the arsonists are vampires?”

Rio snorted at Wanda’s quickly thinning patience, then nodded.

“What’s her basis for that?” Wanda continued. “I have every vampire family in New England documented in my online database—the Smiths, the Kumars, the Wangs. None of them have any fire-related powers. Also, we’re friendly.”

Wanda sent annual holiday postcards to all the significant vampire families in the eastern hemisphere; it was a good way to establish dominance.

“She thinks they’re out-of-townies,” Rio said. “Overseas.”

Wanda shrugged. “I have an alert every time a vampire anywhere in this world takes a plane.”

“You didn’t even know that Agatha was out of town.”

Wanda’s shoulders dipped slightly. That was true; why did Rebecca not tell her? Probably because she didn’t deem it important enough. Wanda had hired her for her discretion, after all.

“I don’t need to keep tabs on my friends.” Lie. But anyways. “Rebecca would tell me if anyone notable had come to Manhattan. She hasn’t.”

“What if it’s someone she didn’t consider notable?” Rio proposed with a shrug. She got up from the chair and started walking to the fridge. “Also, how do you know that every vampire is in your catalogue? I know for a fact you weren’t able to track my movements. I ate all your spies.”

“Yeah, but Agatha didn’t. So I bundled the two of you together.”

“That’s not very precise,” Rio teased. “There were about three times per century that we took a day off from each other. What if I chose then to come kill you?”

“I’d love to see you try.”

“Me too,” Rio said, smiling broadly as she took a blood bottle out of the top shelf of the fridge. “But I wouldn’t want your girlfriend to be mad.”

Wanda was not ready for the overwhelming pulling feeling in her stomach hearing that word being directed at her and Bella.

“Don’t call her that,” Wanda said. It was much weaker than she intended it to be.

To Wanda’s surprise, Bella wasn’t glowing from the attention. In fact she looked a lot less comfortable than she did a moment before. She had her head ducked down, scrolling through her phone.

Wanda couldn’t resist spying, especially when she spotted the familiar iMessage screen. She saw it for only a fraction of a second before Bella clicked the lock button, but she’d had enough time to read the name off—Cassandra.

“Who’s Cassandra?” Wanda asked, because fuck it. “Friend? Secret lover?”

“No one,” Bella said briskly, looking distraught that Wanda had noticed.

“That was one of the names Agatha mentioned,” said Rio simultaneously, giving Bella a thankful smile for jogging her memory. “What a coincidence.”

Wanda fell silent as Rio closed the fridge and started beckoning Nicky from the ceiling, waving the bottle around in the air like it was a fish lure.

As amusing as that display was, Wanda couldn’t help but trail her eyes slowly back to Bella, watching her face very carefully. Her lips were schooled in a straight line, and her gaze was fixed on the floor, studying the carpet.

“Yes…” Wanda trailed off. “What a coincidence.”

But she didn’t push the topic. No, in fact, she showed remarkable restraint, letting the rest of the morning proceed as normally as it could, quietly observing Bella and Rio chatter about trivialities. Unlike Wanda, Rio seemed uninterested in pressing Bella for information. It was a tactic Wanda had never considered, and somehow it coaxed more out of Bella in a single afternoon than Wanda had managed in a month.

They discussed silly things, like their favorite musicians; Rio showed Bella her iPod Nano full of exclusively Joan Jett songs, and Bella told Rio about the Paramore concert she’d seen at Gillette Stadium a few years back. She even showed them a few pictures, which Wanda tried not to stare too intently at.

Bella had pink hair then, and she was wearing a Walpole High School crewneck. A few friends were posed next to her, throwing up peace signs.

It was all incredibly late 2000s.

So she did go to high school there? That timeline makes no sense.

Still, Wanda didn’t dare interrupt. Rio was either the least skeptical person on Earth, or an incredible detective, because the information just kept coming.

“This is Polly, this is Trish, and this is Monica,” Bella said, her gel-tip nail pointing at various teenage faces on her phone screen. “Polly and Trish are both moms now, and Monica’s this big-time lawyer out in Cali. I’d love to visit her sometime. We always promised we’d get matching tattoos once we both landed our dream jobs.”

“It’s great you made so many friends,” Rio said, spooning yogurt into her mouth absentmindedly. “I kind of wish I’d gotten to go to human high school.”

“You still can, if you want,” Bella said, a genuine look of conviction on her face. “I’d be happy to forge you the papers.”

Rio chuckled. “I think I’ll wait until Nicky’s walking and talking before I consider becoming a teen mom.”

“Okay, I’m sorry, but what the hell am I missing?”

Wanda popped their dreamy little bubble with a needle. She had tried to restrain herself, she really had, but she couldn’t help it. She needed to know.

Rio frowned. “I don’t know, what are you missing?”

Wanda felt like she was losing her sanity. “I don’t know, maybe how Bella, a two-thousand year old woman, went to high school in 2014?”

Bella’s face flushed. Wanda had never seen it turn that color. She averted her eyes, closing her phone and swirling the small spoon in her yogurt bowl.

“Um, I think that’s quite obvious,” Rio said, eyes sliding from her to Bella, looking concerned. “Because she wanted to?”

Wanda scoffed, not buying it. “Why would anyone want to go to human high school? In the United States, of all places? You’re lucky if they teach you about something that transpired before George Washington was born.”

Rio looked at her like she was being tone deaf, and Bella sighed. She stood, and her chair scraped unpleasantly against the floor. It was an antique, 1867 from Vienna with steam-bent wood, one that Wanda had let Agatha borrow, and here it had been reduced to a kitchen effigy, stained with applesauce.

“What? Am I wrong?” Wanda mumbled, but she was being thoroughly ignored.

“Thank you for the breakfast, Rio,” Bella said. “It was a pleasure meeting you. If you ever feel compelled to murder me in the future, I hope our brief companionship deters you from it, or at least gives you a bit of hesitation. I’ll wash and iron your clothes and get them back to you tomorrow.”

Rio laughed, rising as well to shake her hand.

“Ironing isn’t necessary.”

“Oh, good. Because I don’t own one.”

They shared another laugh and Wanda felt a brief and furious jealousy. It wasn’t that they couldn’t be friends—Wanda could share—but why did she feel like she was on the outside of something?

Wanda said goodbye next, and it all felt so procedural. Bella grabbed her coat, and shucked on a tote bag. She held the door for Bella to walk out, and they made their way down the stairs, each of their footfalls timed one after another, so Bella was always a step too far ahead.

Wanda shut the clamshell door of the apartment behind them. The air outside was humid and miserable, and Wanda’s first instinct was to call a car. She dug into her pocket for her phone, but before she could ring Rebecca, Bella was already walking.

“Where are you going?” Wanda called after her.

“Feel free to take the car,” Bella shouted into the wind. “I need some fresh air.”

Wanda felt like her stomach was tied on a string, and Bella was tugging it along the pavement. There was no other option; of course she was going to follow her. Bella regarded her decision with a glance as Wanda jogged up.

“You consider this fresh?” Wanda said. “It feels like we’re inside an aquarium.”

Bella didn’t reply. A car rushed past them, spraying the sidewalk with dirt, and normally Bella would dodge out of the way, but she just singlemindedly walked forward, letting the water soak into the hem of her jeans.

“Bella, did I offend you?” Wanda asked, heaving out an exasperated breath. “Well, of course I did. But can you spell it out for me, just this once? I feel like my foot lives in my mouth whenever I’m around you.”

Bella let out a laugh, but it was dry and humorless.

“You didn’t offend me. Really.”

It was terribly hard to know if she was telling the truth with such a limited view of her facial features. She was only allowing Wanda to see the side of it, and as usual it was perfectly manicured, her lips not quite a smile or a frown.

Wanda sighed. She stuck her hands in her pockets and looked up towards the sky. Even in the heat, it was a beautiful blue. Maybe it was even more beautiful because of the heat; it had this effect of making it look like a messy watercolor painting.

Wanda felt similarly about her life now, like a bunch of indistinct shapes flailing around. Everything had been so clean-cut before: career (important), Billy (important), personal life (nonexistent, will get to later.) But then there was Bella, and they barely knew each other, but Bella was smart, exceptionally so, and she was witty, charismatic, and now she was a vampire too and—she walked much too fast.

“Can you slow down?”

“I’m twice your age, Wanda. Shouldn’t you be doing laps around me?”

“Bella.”

“Yes?”

Bella allowed Wanda another sliver of her face. Just a tenth of it, a slight look to the left. But it was enough to urge Wanda on.

“Look, I’m sorry if I’ve been abrasive,” she said. “And overbearing. And probably, on the whole, quite unpleasant to be around.”

That tore a short laugh out of her walking companion—and lowered her speed by a few seconds. It was enough for Wanda to catch her breath.

“It’s just—it’s been a very long time since I’ve had a friend,” she continued, throat pinching with embarrassment at such a juvenile confession, and Bella raised an eyebrow; Wanda read her meaning. “Agatha doesn’t count. She’s more like an old purse that I can’t throw away. And, well, she hasn’t died on me yet. That’s mainly why.”

The edge of Bella's lip tugged up. Wanda’s chest tightened. She knew what she wanted to say, but it felt so small and fragile in her chest.

“Look—when I was younger, after my parents and my brother passed, this human family briefly took me in. They had a girl who was roughly my age. Tabitha. She would make these quite impressive dolls out of straw, and we’d go up into the hayloft and play out these ridiculous scenarios; things like the family cow beginning to talk, or her father inheriting great wealth and buying Tabitha and I new dresses.”

The tug at Bella’s lips had become a full-fledged smile. It was such a contrast to the horrible dread Wanda felt at dredging up the memory.

“During the summer, Tabitha went off to her grandmother’s. I wasn’t allowed to come, because her grandmother only had one extra bed. I hated being left alone. It terrified me, it made me so angry—” Wanda took a breath in, “—that when Tabitha returned, I gave her a nightmare so bad that she ran into the woods, crying and screaming. She was lost in there for an entire weekend.”

Wanda glanced at Bella’s face, waiting for it to fall; waiting for the inevitable disgust. But her expression remained remarkably unmoved.

“That’s unfortunate,” Bella said. Wanda agreed. “Did you give her the nightmare on purpose?”

Wanda blinked. She’d never been asked that before, nor had she considered it.

“I can’t remember,” Wanda said. “But it feels as if I did. I must have. They kicked me out after that, and I lived by myself for a very long time. I’ve had friends here and there since, but when I lose my temper, I tend to…”

Wanda hovered her hands an inch from her skull, then mimicked an explosion.

“Do your powers actually kill people?” Bella asked instead.

“No, not usually,” Wanda said. “They just slowly drive them insane.”

“Huh. I must be the odd one out, then, because I feel like knowing you has made me feel more normal than I ever have before.”

Wanda glanced up at her, startled. But Bella was looking to the side, at a lake, where several ducklings were chasing after their darting mother. Wanda wasn’t sure when they’d started parading through Central Park; it was usually a place she typically avoided at all costs, as it was always so packed with people living lives so apart from her own, but it was different with Bella. They blended in.

“How so?” Wanda ventured, hoping she didn’t sound as desperate as she felt.

Bella turned toward her, and smiled. It wasn’t forced, or pitiful.

“You know when you move to a new city, and suddenly no one knows about that time in first grade when you cried in front of the class?”

Wanda squinted at her, feeling lost. “Not particularly.”

Bella laughed. “Not that exact scenario. I just mean the feeling of it. Of being brand new.”

They came to the edge of the pond, and Bella knelt down by the ridge of it, silently inviting Wanda to join her. So she did.

“Like reinventing yourself?” Wanda asked, shouting above the noise. They were surrounded on all sides by a variety of ruckus: a newsstand, a hot dog seller, a bride and a groom and their wedding photographer, and a gaggle of children chasing between and around all of them. It was so loud that Wanda had to reluctantly move closer to hear Bella, the two of them pressed knee to knee.

“Kind of,” Bella said, looking between them, where their legs touched. “I spent the vast majority of my life living in a village so small that the limits of my imagination were restricted to a precise few acres of land, and the opinions of the forty or so people living on that land. Like, the woman who cut my hair said I liked my hair cut short, but really it was just more convenient for her to cut it that way. That sort of thing, you know?”

Bella ran her fingers through her hair; it was long enough now that it splayed over her collarbones.

“I have to admit, I don’t,” Wanda said quietly. “I was blessed enough to become an orphan in a city, not on a farm.”

Bella laughed, her eyes squinting. Wanda thought that might have been an unempathetic thing to say, but it didn’t seem to land that way.

“I wished so many times that I would wake up an orphan in a city, any city,” Bella whispered. “I wanted so badly to attend some sort of school. I was taught very little, only Latin, because it was the tongue of the nobles, and my mother wanted me to be able to converse with them. Logic and mathematics and science were all far away concepts, basically fairy tales. My mother didn’t view learning as pertinent to my job.”

Wanda wanted to ask what she meant by her job, but she didn’t press.

“So when I got the opportunity to come here,” Bella trailed off, taking a breath in. “To Boston. I fed this local librarian a whole half-fabricated sob story, and she gave me a place to stay, and she tutored me until I was ready to enroll in the local high school in Walpole. And God, once I got there, it was… exhilarating. My classmates were so encouraging, and brightminded—far more brightminded than any vampires I knew, who were two millennia their senior—and I learned so much so quickly. I was a sponge. And I had fun. I was even the prom queen senior year.”

“Of course you were.”

Bella snickered.

“Of course, I was always aware that I wasn’t actually a child, and these people weren’t my contemporaries. But it didn’t matter. For the first time in my life, I actually felt like I was growing into something instead of running into the rigid edges of my cage.”

Wanda’s heart strained, thinking back to that nightmare. Had Bella really spent two years trapped in a broom closet? And worse even, had it happened multiple times? The more she relived the memory—smelled the dust and felt the anger and the fear in Bella’s young hands pressed to the locked door—the more furious she became. But all that trapped resentment puffed out like a cigarette when Bella began speaking again.

“And it was so funny, honestly, how some of the things they were going through—crushes, unfavorable parenting, poor courtship attempts from boys—I had lived through a hundred times. But with other things, like figuring out what to do with their short lives, it felt like these seventeen year olds had much more wisdom than me. They were so certain in their direction. They felt as if nothing inhibited them. It made me feel silly for living two thousand years without having a plan.”

“But then I learned about you,” Bella said, her hands settling onto Wanda’s knee. “I’d known about you vaguely before—my mother likes to gossip—but one of my science teachers mentioned your research in class, how he’d just come from a conference you’d been speaking at. He couldn’t shut up about how magnetic of a speaker you were. And gosh—once I put two and two together about who you were, I couldn’t get my mind off of you. A vampire who cared about something other than themselves. Frankly, you sounded impossible.”

Wanda snorted, covering her face with her hands. Bella pried them open, then took them in her own, intertwining them.

“And then I met you. And contrary to everything I’d experienced, you were exactly like I imagined you to be.”

“Arrogant?” Wanda said stiltedly, hoping to distract from the rapid beating of her heart that Bella surely could hear. “Self-absorbed?”

“No. Unbelievably charitable and kind,” Bella said instead, and it made Wanda feel like a ship that had just been hit on several sides, and was now rapidly losing water. “First you invited me to live with you, and then you came to my lecture, you liked my lecture—and god. I told myself I’d work with you because it was the fastest way to solve my own problems, but really—I think I just really needed to be around a vampire who saw me as… intelligent. An equal. I don’t want you to think about who I was before I got here.”

“Bella…” Wanda’s heart clenched. When she didn’t answer immediately, Bella covered her face and groaned, as if she was embarrassed with herself.

“Which is why I’ve been so secretive. And cocky. And overzealous. I’m not here to destroy you. I just wanted to impress you,” she said, her voice muffled by the palms of her hands. “And it’s not exactly impressive to say that I learned algebra for the first time at the age of two thousand and fifteen.”

This time, it was Wanda prying her fingers from her face. And Bella never looked more beautiful than then, her eyes a little watery, her lipstick a little smudged.

It was all too much.

“I don’t care when you learned algebra, Bella,” she whispered.

Bella laughed once, and wiped her wet cheek. “You don’t?”

“This might surprise you, but that is not the barometer I use to decide if I’m going to be friends with someone or not.”

And as stupid and childish and silly as Wanda felt, talking about friendship and holding hands in Central Park like a couple of twenty-somethings on a first Tinder date, it was all worth it for the wolfish, shit-eating grin she got in return.

“So we are friends.”

“Don’t push it,” Wanda warned. “I’m letting it happen. Trying something new.”

“Does this mean I get to take all your money now, interest-free?”

Wanda rolled her eyes. “No. I only have room for one leech.”

Bella laughed, and wrapped Wanda in a hug that took her by surprise.

“I’ll just have to find my own unique lane, then,” she said quietly into Wanda’s ear, just to torment her.

Wanda’s hands froze by her sides, taking in a breath before lightly reciprocating, patting her once on the back.

“I think you’re already carving one,” Wanda muttered. “With a shovel and a pickaxe.”

Bella laughed; she was about to say something when her phone vibrated, and Wanda saw the name flutter across the screen again. Cassandra.

It wasn’t a text message this time.

“I… I should take this,” Bella said, a fear and an urgency in her voice where it hadn’t existed a moment ago. “Meet you back at school?”

Wanda stared for a moment at Bella, and suddenly remembered back when she was a kid, and Pietro would hang over the side of the bed; his face had looked so strange upside down, his lips uneven and too large, his nose misplaced. It was as if he was an alien doing an impersonation of a human being.

Back then, she’d pushed him off the bed, and told him to never do it again—it made her feel scared—but now, she didn’t say anything. She just held it in her chest.

“Sure,” she eventually said with a nod. “Say hi for me, to whoever that is.”

Chapter 20

If Wanda was any other person, having her house set on fire probably would have distracted her from her work, at least for the few days. But it didn’t. After she ensured that Billy was safe, she barely even registered that it had happened at all.

It might seem strange that she was so unbothered, but it was helpful to remember that Wanda had been threatened by vampires her entire life. Even Evanorah Harkness had tried—and failed—to have her assassinated. And if Evanorah couldn’t kill you, who could?

That was how she and Agatha met, actually.

It was a few years before Evanorah’s death, at the ripe beginning of the millenium. Wanda was still just scraping by back then, offering her services as a fortune teller to various barons. At the time, a nobleman named Harrald Weiss kept her particularly employed.

He was the paranoid type—always the most lucrative—which meant he would consult with Wanda daily to figure out which of his men was planning on betraying him. In reality, it was none of them, but Wanda made a lot of money pointing fingers and sending heads to the guillotine. It weighed on her conscience a bit every time, but this was before she’d had enough money to afford developing a moral backbone.

But consequences did eventually catch up to her, as they tended to do.

One of the men she accused of being a backstabber turned out to be a vampire who was seeking to enthrall Weiss. A Harkness Clan member—not that she knew that then. Besides the vampires who’d turned her and killed her family, she was acquainted with none, and that was by design. She naturally despised them. But she wasn’t exactly trying to invoke their wrath, either.

Wrath came anyway, in the form of Agatha Harkness. Agatha had been even sloppier back then than she was now. She’d snuck into Wanda’s room in the nighttime, only snuck was not the right word—it was more like slammed, or crashed, or rummaged. Her hip bumped into Wanda’s desk, her elbow knocked over her candlestick, and her feet tripped over a stack of books.

Naturally, Wanda’s eyes flew open a second later, and Agatha cursed. She came at Wanda with a stake, but halted midway. Her shoulders went limp, then her knees, then her eyes hollowed. The usual procedure. To her credit, she fought off the Nightmare three times, each time getting closer to Wanda before falling into it again, as if they were playing a game of Red Light, Green Light. At some point it became so ridiculous that Wanda started laughing.

“What’s so funny?” Agatha had groaned, sounding more annoyed than furious. After a few more failed attempts, she gave up and sat on the floor by Wanda’s bed, completely depleted. She took a ten minute nap, then asked Wanda if she had anything to drink, preferably wine. Wanda should have tried to kill her in retaliation, but she found something about the woman’s audaciousness weirdly charming. So they struck up a conversation instead.

They ended up having more in common than they anticipated. They both hated vampires, had strong opinions about politics, and were looking forward to the day where women had more rights than horses. They agreed that Agatha would go back to her mother with the news that Wanda had begged for forgiveness and pledged allegiance to the Harkness Clan.

That plan failed. Evanorah deemed her daughter useless—what a surprise—and sent three more mercenaries after Wanda. All their murder attempts were equally fruitless. If anything, they helped Wanda’s career. She rounded them up, convinced Weiss that they were assassins there to kill him, and he got to execute more people. Eventually, Evanorah gave up, then she died.

Thank you Rio.

So she was used to a little violence from the immortal riff-raff. Still, she made sure to assign Rebecca on the task of figuring out who was responsible, and if that didn’t pan out, she also pre-emptively sent out an invitation to all the vampire families in the Northeast to come to her Albany compound for dinner. She figured if nothing else, she could discern who was trying to kill her over a nice dinner of steak and potatoes.

This was all to say, her research finally had her undivided attention. It might as well have been a miracle. For the first time since Wanda had agreed to let Bella into her lab, they were actually–finally–getting somewhere.

It all came together fairly quickly after their chat by the lake. Since Wanda’s lab was full of sea creatures, she was able to secure a temporary hold on Waldorf’s lab to conduct the fungus research. There had been quite a few vacant lab spaces available, but his was outfitted with the exact instruments Wanda would need to test Bella’s theory—high-resolution microscopes, centrifuges, live-cell imaging equipment—and besides that, sending him that e-mail was so satisfying.

It took two days before he finally gave up the fight, and another two to move his research materials elsewhere. That meant, by the time they had finally gotten their coats on the hanger, Bella had twenty-six days left to effectively solve immortality.

Not that she was acting like it.

“You’re late,” Wanda said when she heard the door to the lab shutter. She glanced up at the clock on the wall. Nine forty-three A.M. “Three minutes worse than yesterday.”

Bella strolled in, shucked on her lab coat, and bent down so she was eye level with Wanda, who was already an hour deep into analyzing slides.

“New personal best,” she said, her tone exuding zero guilt. “But I promise I have a good explanation this time. I was walking to work when…”

“Please spare me the details.”

“...I saw a poster outside of Kwala Coffee advertising their new birthday cake latte. Apparently the owner’s daughter came up with the recipe. She’s, like, nine years old. A coffee prodigy. What was I going to do? Not try it? But there was a long line.”

The sheer ludicrousness of this excuse forced Wanda to look up from her slides.

“Your emergency is that you were emotionally manipulated by a poster?”

Bella huffed, kicking her shoes off before hopping on the examination table to Wanda’s right. Wanda had prepped it already, a roll of waxy paper crumpling underneath Bella’s fingers.

“I wasn’t manipulated. I acted in accordance with my values.” Bella rolled up her sleeve so her arm was exposed. “It’s my moral imperative to support female entrepreneurs.”

That forced a laugh out of Wanda. She got out of her chair, snapped on a new pair of nitrile gloves, and picked out a clean collection tube.

“The child is not an entrepreneur because she put sprinkles in coffee,” Wanda said, laying her hand on Bella’s arm. “That’s an unsustainably low bar for innovation.”

Bella just smiled at her, dropping their sham of an argument with a clipped laugh. Despite Wanda’s efforts to keep their discussion light and stupid—and that it very much was—her voice fell frustratingly quiet once she was touching Bella’s skin.

It was such a clinical thing, taking someone’s blood. Wanda had done it a hundred times over during her temporary stint as a phlebotomist, and it had never once felt sexy, even for someone who belonged to a species of blood worshippers. People’s veins were finicky, and you had to pay attention to get the needle in right. It was an exacting task that required focus.

And yet all Wanda could focus on was keeping her heartrate down.

Because despite a somewhat successful first day in the lab yesterday—and Wanda’s commitment to using the word friends to describe their tenuous relationship—shifting their dynamic towards something professional was not without its growing pains.

They had—at least in Wanda’s head—made an implicit agreement that they would start focusing on the work, and put whatever sexual chemistry they had to the side. But somehow trying to not think about it made it so much worse.

“You put the needle in the vein,” Bella said, cutting into Wanda’s internal monologue. She had the audacity to look at Wanda like she was an amnesia patient. “And then blood comes out.”

“Shut up,” Wanda muttered, inserting the needle without warning. Unforgivably, Bella didn’t flinch; she just looked Wanda straight in the eyes, like a psychopath.

“I’m your patient, Wanda. Have some decorum.”

Wanda watched the tube fill up with blood, averting her gaze from Bella. “I don’t have decorum. Why do you think I got fired from the hospital I worked at?”

“You worked at a hospital?” Bella dropped the scandalized act as quickly as she adopted it. She would have made a phenomenal actress. “Why would you ever need to work anywhere?”

“I wanted the experience of working with human biology. I thought it’d be useful in case I ever needed to patch up Billy—”

Wanda froze, staring at the collection tube as her mouth went slack. She saw Bella’s reflection in the glass; she was narrowing her eyes curiously.

“Billy,” Bella repeated. “Who is Billy?”

Shit. Shit. Shit.

Even though she’d started to trust Bella more since their last conversation—whether that was because she had actually divulged something personal, or simply because Wanda was exhausted by her own paranoia, she wasn’t sure—but still she didn’t feel like she could just tell Bella about her son. It was like handing her a gun and a silver bullet.

“My nephew,” she settled on.

“Ah. Right.” She saw Bella’s eyes dip to something on the floor a few feet away. She got off the examination table, and reached for her tote bag. “Speaking of.”

Bella extracted something from the bag, then held it up; it was a photoframe. Specifically, the photo of Wanda and Billy that had been sitting on Wanda’s display table back at home. She’d assumed it’d been lost in the fire.

Unable to help it, Wanda’s fingers curled around the frame protectively. There was a single crack down the middle, but the image inside was preserved.

“I went back for it,” Bella said. “Just in case you didn’t have a backup.”

Of course, Wanda did have a backup; she had every photograph taken of Billy digitally and physically archived, but her chest got unhelpfully heavy from the sentimentality of the gesture.

“That’s… sweet of you,” Wanda said, trying and failing to school her face into apathy. She swallowed down the thickness in her throat. “But you really didn’t have to.”

Bella reached out and held Wanda’s hand, then shrugged.

“You’re someone who’s worth going out of my way for.”

Wanda’s chest tightened. For once, Bella’s nails were bare, untouched by polish or extensions. Her face was the same. No lipstick, just the barest amount of foundation painted over a bruise on her neck. Wanda didn’t put much stock in the equivalencies human men tended to make between makeup and honesty, but it was nice to see her like this— vulnerable.

Even if it was only for the sake of avoiding cross-contamination.

“Anyway,” Bella said, intertwining their fingers and pointing their conjoined hands towards the blood sample Wanda had abandoned. “That one’s already a goner, probably.”

Wanda flinched, returning to reality.“Damn it.” She dropped Bella’s hand, ignoring the way the other woman’s typically invincible smile dipped at the side.

Trying to react quickly, she scrambled for the collection tube. She drew a small amount of blood out of it before transferring a single droplet onto a glass side. Then she smeared the top with another slide so that the cells were all on one clean observable layer.

Ducking under the sterile hood, she moved to the microscope, haphazardly adjusting the knobs.

The image of Bella’s blood came into focus quickly, but the cells were already deteriorating. “Hell’s sake.” Her grip around the microscope tightened with frustration.

They’d taken almost thirty blood samples from Bella since they began yesterday, and not a single one had survived exposure to the outside environment. In itself, that had been a fascinating revelation; blood wasn’t supposed to rapidly break down when it left the body. But if they were going to find this hypothetical fungus, they needed an actual sample they could analyze.

Wanda gave Bella a harried look, and Bella laughed, understanding it immediately. She laid back down and rolled up her sleeve. They repeated the process, only this time, Wanda didn’t let herself get distracted. She already had the microscope set to the correct focus in advance, so she could slide the glass in as quickly as possible without sending it flying. It came into focus immediately, and, ah, ah—

She slammed her fist onto the table.

“I saw something!”

“You did?”

“Yes. It was there for like half a second, but I swear. There were little spindly fibers.”

“Oh my god. You mean—”

“Hyphae,” Wanda said breathlessly.

“Hyphae!”

Bella tugged Wanda’s rolly chair forward, folding her into a hug. Wanda was too excited to shrug her off, even if this was the most ridiculous thing she’d ever embraced someone over. Hyphae were the tiny, thread-like parts that made up fungi. Red blood cells were round, uniform; they didn’t have any stringy bits.

Spotting hyphae with a microscope was no real marvel, but spotting it under these circumstances was.

For one, fungi shouldn’t have been so easily visible on an unstained slide; and two, the only time fungi ever entered the bloodstream was during something like fungemia, and if Bella had fungemia, she wouldn’t be laughing and kicking her feet painfully into Wanda’s ribs.

Bella nudged her nose against Wanda’s ear. “I told you so.”

Wanda shuddered, and finally had the wherewithal to push her away.

“Bella,” she said, holding her gaze. “We need to set some… boundaries.”

“Oh, finally. I was wondering when this conversation would happen.”

Wanda’s eyebrows rose, her steely face faltering.

“What conversation?”

“This one. That we’re about to have,” Bella said, tongue poking into her cheek. “About how you want me to stop flirting with you.”

Wanda’s cheeks flushed. God, was this woman always ten infuriating steps ahead of her? “Well. That’s not exactly…” She stuttered. “Actually, yes. You’re right. That is the heart of it.”

“Let me do some more guesswork. It’s because you think it makes us unproductive,” Bella continued mechanically, as if they were going over a contract that she’d already read ten times over. “And now that you know I’m a vampire, the adrenaline-pumping appeal of hooking up with your younger human post-doc has disappeared. Am I correct?”

At that, Wanda scoffed. “Okay—no. You’re not. You being younger and a post-doc were the main reasons I was avoiding it to begin with. It’s much more appealing…” Wanda’s words trailed off as she realized the grave mistake she was making, but the hole was already dug. “...Now.”

Bella’s cocky grin melted off her face. Her knuckles whitened where they held the edge of the exam table.

From her expression, it looked as if she hadn’t considered the possibility that Wanda was still attracted to her. How could she have possibly assumed that? It wasn’t like Bella had suddenly stopped looking like a Victoria Secret model.

“Now?” Bella said quietly, a frog in her throat. “Please elaborate.”

Wanda covered her face in embarrassment. “I’d rather not.”

“You’re the one who started this conversation.”

“And now I’d like to be the one to end it, thank you.”

Bella grabbed her wrist, forcibly but gently peeling her hand from her forehead.

“Hey,” she said, pulling Wanda’s hand to her mouth and pressing her lips to it softly. It was jolting. “I’m a big girl. I can handle rejection. Just tell me.”

“I’m not—this isn’t a rejection,” Wanda breathed out. Her chest felt like a stove burner that had been left on. “We were just hooking up. Having sex. Which was great, I’m not saying it wasn’t, but now it poses a distraction to the real reason we’re here, so it’s best if we just drop it. I’m sure you’ve had this kind of tryst with… countless vampires. These arrangements come and go.”

“Countless vampires? That’s a bit presumptive.”

“You’re built like a siren. It’s not presumptive at all.”

At that, Bella cackled, squeezing Wanda’s hand hard. It hurt, but not in a bad way.

“God, you make me laugh.” There was an affection in Bella’s voice that made Wanda tremble a little. “You make it sound like you’ve never hooked up with a vampire before.”

Had she really made it that obvious? Oh, god. Wanda couldn’t meet her eyes, squeezing them shut instead as her skin burned with embarrassment.

“Wait.” Bella’s lips snapped open. “No way.”

“Yes, well—humans are far less complicated.”

“Wanda. Never?”

Wanda shrugged.

“Oh my god,” Bella exhaled. “You should have never told me that. Now I absolutely can’t honor this breakup. There is so much you’re missing out on.”

It was the word breakup that finally pushed her over the edge. Not the word itself, but the fact that Wanda knew she was using it for comedic effect.

“I don’t like it when you joke about that.”

She immediately regretted saying it. Regretted it more when Bella’s face fell. Wanda tried to turn away from her, but Bella gripped her waist, fixing her there.

“Joke about what?”

“Everything you said while we were at Rio’s apartment. Calling me your girlfriend. Now, talking about us breaking up, as if we were ever an item. It’s not funny to me. It’s… confusing.”

Wanda had no idea where she was going with this, or why she felt so defensive about it. All she’d meant to do was put an end to the needless petting and hand holding—things that did not belong in a scientific environment—and now words were falling out of her mouth uselessly like fish flopping out of a barrel.

Wanda hoped Bella might see her discomfort and drop the topic, but instead she studied Wanda’s face with a great and sudden intensity. Her eyes were like a loaded bow.

They were the same in that way, horrible at letting things go.

“I apologize.”

Wanda began to retreat immediately. “That’s not necessary. Really, let’s just drop it.”

“It is necessary. Like I said before, back at your apartment—I don’t think it’s funny to give people the wrong impression. I think it’s one of the worst things you can do to someone. Really.”

Wanda’s face burned, feeling an unfamiliar feeling of rejection pinch at her stomach. “You weren’t giving me the wrong impression. I know exactly the nature of our relationship. We’re friendly. We’re coworkers. We’ve slept together. You think I’m attractive, I think you’re attractive. We have a mutual intellectual respect. That’s all there is to it. I understand why the jokes were funny, I just… I’d rather we not play around with it. We don’t have to make it into a whole thing.”

Bella shook her head and frowned.

“See, I did give you the wrong impression.”

Wanda’s brow creased. “What? How?”

“I wasn’t teasing you like that because I thought our relationship was something trivial,” Bella said. “It was… wishful thinking, on my behalf. Projection.”

“...What?” Projection? “I don’t understand.”

“It’s embarrassing, but… I’ve never actually been with someone,”—from the way she emphasized the word, Wanda assumed she meant intimately—“and actually found it pleasurable. It’s always been very transactional for me. I think… selfishly, I want to hang onto that pleasure for as long as possible, and I get threatened when you try to take it away. But of course, that’s not your fault. It’s just been a long time since I’ve been able to enjoy my own body.”

“Transactional? Bella, do you mean…”

Bella had been homeless. She had money issues. And it wasn’t like she could just Suggest money out of people like Agatha.

“No, I’m not a prostitute.” Bella smiled. “At least not in the way you’re probably thinking. But my job back home wasn’t completely different. My body was very much an asset to the work.”

There was it again—that job. From the unspoken before times.

“What was your job?”

Bella smiled sadly, then reached out to push a lock of misbehaving hair behind Wanda’s ear. Her touch was like the sting of a bee. All Wanda could hear in her ears was white noise.

“Stealing,” she admitted, finally, with a tight smile. “From noblemen.”

“Stealing? Like what, their possessions? Selling them for money?”

“No. Stealing their life force. Like, their souls.” Bella sighed. “And giving them to my mother.”

Chapter 21

Wanda blinked, stupefied by what she’d just heard.

“Life force,” she repeated. It felt like such an unusually grand way to say it. “Do you mean their blood? You removed their blood… and gave it to your mother?”

In all of Wanda’s many years of vampiric research, she’d very rarely encountered a story about a vampire stealing blood to give to someone else.

Mostly because it was impractical.

Feeding was the vampire’s natural instinct, and for most of history there were no good alternatives. Unless Bella had been draining victims with a syringe. But they didn’t have the same cooling and storing technology back then that they did now. The blood would be clotted and rancid by the time she could give it to her mother.

Bella shook her head.

“No, no. Not blood.” Bella pressed her hand to her chest. “Their life force.”

Wanda leaned back in her chair, cocking her head to the side.

“Like, their organs?”

Bella laughed. “God, no. Ew. I have no use for a man’s liver.”

“Then what?” Wanda said with exasperation. “I have no clue what you’re talking about.”

“That’s fair. You’re too young to understand.”

Wanda pinned her with a searing glare. Bella grinned innocuously.

“I’m a thousand years old, Bella,” she muttered. “I’m sure I have the faculties.”

Bella laughed again. “Not like that. I just mean, you’re literally too young. Or should I say, too evolved.” She paused. “Where do you keep your library?”

Wanda let out a baffled shock of laughter. “My library?”

“You’re a total hoarder. Don’t tell me you don’t have the entirety of the vampire canon assembled in a basement somewhere.”

Wanda grimaced, and Bella laughed.

She did, indeed—although it wasn’t in a basement. All the most important primary and secondary sources she’d found throughout the years were stored in a highly-protected underground facility in Pennsylvania.

Reading her face, Bella shook her head, delighted. “I knew it. Can you have some things shipped here?”

“Shipping won’t be necessary,” Wanda said, then turned towards her computer monitor. She started typing in the password to her online database. “I have everything digitized.”

A hand laid over Wanda’s on the keyboard, stopping her before she could hit enter.

“It’ll be easier with the real copy,” Bella said. “Trust me.”

Wanda studied her for a long moment, but Bella’s tight smile revealed nothing.

“Fine,” Wanda said with a shrug, extracting her fingers from underneath Bella’s hands before she started enjoying it too much. “I can have anything you want overnighted.”

“Overnighted.” Bella fanned her face. “You make that sound sexy somehow.”

“Bella.”

“Wanda.”

Bella’s eyes lingered on Wanda’s lips. Wanda wanted nothing more than to climb on top of that examination table and kiss her stupid, overly secretive face senseless.

“I wish you were uglier,” Wanda muttered, and Bella’s eyebrows flew up. It was nice to finally startle her, for once. “Come on. I want to show you my new toys.”

***

To Bella’s great dismay, toys did not mean what she was hoping they would. But million- dollar science equipment was a nice second best.

As they continued their work, the rest of the day swam by, five hours passing right under Bella’s nose.

And despite the lingering background terror that she had felt ever since she knew her family had arrived early, Bella was having the most fun she’d ever had—probably in her entire life. Not that there was much fun to compete with.

Working with Wanda was just so addicting. Once you got the woman over a mental roadblock, she was like a meat grinder. She talked a mile a minute, developing novel theories then abandoning them in the span of thirty seconds. Then, just as you thought you’d caught up to her line of thinking, she’d freeze, lift her goggles up—so you could see the adorable red rims they’d left around her eyes—then she’d nod to herself, and slide them back on.

It was how you’d know she’d just had a breakthrough.

Five hours. That’s how long it took for them to make more progress than Bella assumed many scientists made in their entire career.
